                If patient currently taking ticagrelor (Brilinta®), contact Medical Direction for

                    Classification: Ester-type local anesthetic (topical)

Pediatric       LOCAL ANESTHESIA TO FACILITATE ENDOTRACHEAL INTUBATION:

                 Causes a reversible blockade of nerve conduction by decreasing nerve
                  membrane permeability to sodium. This decreases the rate of membrane
Action(s)         depolarization, thereby increasing the threshold for electrical excitability.
                  The blockade affects all nerve fibers in the following sequence: autonomic,
                  sensory, and motor, with effects diminishing in reverse order.

                  DISEASE:
Route
                  0.5 mg / 2.5 mg in 3 mL or 0.5 mg / 3 mg in 3 mL

Pediatric Dose / REACTIVE LOWER AIRWAY DISEASE / ASTHMA:
Route            0.5 mg / 2.5 mg in 3 mL or 0.5 mg / 3 mg in 3 mL
                  Simultaneous administration of both an anticholinergic drug and a beta-2
                   agonist produces a greater bronchodilator effect
                  Ipratropium antagonizes the action of acetylcholine by blocking muscarinic
                   cholinergic receptors that are present in the airways and other body
                   organs. The actions of ipratropium parallel those of atropine on the
                   bronchial smooth muscle, salivary glands, GI tract, and heart when
                   administered by the intravenous route. When administered by oral
Action(s)          inhalation, however, ipratropium exhibits greater antimuscarinic activity on
                   the bronchial smooth muscle; systemic effects are minimal.
                  Albuterol is a moderately selective beta2-adrenergic agonist that stimulates
                   receptors of the smooth muscle in the lungs, uterus, and vasculature
                   supplying skeletal muscle. The net result of albuterol binding to beta2-
                   receptors in the lungs is relaxation of bronchial smooth muscles, which in
                   turn relieves bronchospasm, reduces airway resistance, facilitates mucous
                   drainage, and increases vital capacity.
                  Wheezing associated with
                   o Asthma
                   o Bronchitis with bronchospasm
Indications        o COPD
                   o Epiglottitis
                   o Allergic reaction / anaphylaxis
                   o Inhalation burns
                  Angioedema
                  Laryngomalacia
Contra-           Hypersensitivity to albuterol or levalbuterol.
indications       Use with extreme caution in patients taking MAOIs
                  Use with caution in lactating patients, or patients with cardiovascular
                   disease history
                  Dizziness
                  Tachycardia
                  Blurred vision
                  Dry mouth
Side Effects
                  Hypertension
                  Paradoxical bronchospasm

                   After IV administration, sodium bicarbonate dissociates to bicarbonate
                    ions, which make up the base portion of the body’s extracellular buffer
                    system. Restores acid-base balance in patients with metabolic or
                    respiratory acidosis, however resulting metabolic alkalosis can occur.
Action(s)
                    Excess bicarbonate ions are excreted in urine, alkalizing the urine.
                    Alkalization decreases renal absorption and increases the clearance of
                    certain drugs, intoxicants, and weak acids.
                   Slows uptake of cyclic antidepressants.
                   Cyclic antidepressant / sodium channel blocker overdose
                   Hyperkalemia
Indications

                   Crush injuries, suspension injuries, entrapment
Contra-
                   None when used as indicated.
indications

Side Effects       Metabolic alkalosis
                   Extravasation can lead to tissue necrosis
NOTE: It is important to ensure prolonged adequacy of ventilation in an attempt to mitigate
respiratory acidosis before using sodium bicarbonate in cardiac arrest.

                NOT APPROVED FOR PEDIATRIC USE
Dose / Route
                 Hemostatic agent that binds to fibrin on the plasminogen/plasmin molecule.
                  Prevents the binding plasminogen to fibrin, preserving and stabilizing the
Action(s)
                  matrix of fibrin, subsequently diminishing the ability of plasmin to lyse fibrin
                  clots.

                 Suspected or known hemorrhage with hypotension (90mmHg) AND arrival
Indications
                  time at a Level 1 Trauma Center >30 minutes from onset of injury.

                 Hypersensitivity
Contra-
                 Known subarachnoid or intracranial hemorrhage
indications
                 Administration >3 HOURS from onset of injury

                 Cerebral infarct
                 Headache
Side Effects     Seizure
                 Hypotension
                 Arterial or Venous thrombosis

Note: Tranexamic acid must NOT be administered greater than 3 hours after onset of
injury. Serious adverse effects can occur.
